```
"""Plan-aware queue progress and frozen score display helpers."""

from __future__ import annotations

from desloppify.core.exception_sets import PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS
from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize


def plan_aware_queue_count(state: dict, plan: dict | None = None) -> int:
    """Count remaining plan-aware queue items (skips excluded, clusters collapsed)."""
    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue

    result = build_work_queue(
        state,
        options=QueueBuildOptions(
            status="open",
            count=None,
            plan=plan,
            collapse_clusters=True,
            include_skipped=False,
        ),
    )
    return result["total"]


def get_plan_start_strict(plan: dict | None) -> float | None:
    """Extract the frozen plan-start strict score, or None if unset."""
    if not plan:
        return None
    return plan.get("plan_start_scores", {}).get("strict")


def print_frozen_score_with_queue_context(
    plan: dict,
    queue_remaining: int,
) -> None:
    """Show frozen plan-start score + queue progress."""
    scores = plan.get("plan_start_scores", {})
    strict = scores.get("strict")
    if strict is None:
        return
    print(
        colorize(
            f"\n  Score (frozen at plan start): strict {strict:.1f}/100",
            "cyan",
        )
    )
    print(
        colorize(
            f"  Queue: {queue_remaining} item{'s' if queue_remaining != 1 else ''}"
            " remaining. Score will not update until the queue is clear and you run `desloppify scan`.",
            "dim",
        )
    )


def print_execution_or_reveal(
    state: dict,
    prev,
    plan: dict | None,
) -> None:
    """Context-aware score display: frozen plan-start score or live scores.

    If a plan has ``plan_start_scores`` and there are queue items remaining,
    show the frozen score + queue progress.  Otherwise delegate to the
    existing live ``print_score_update``.
    """
    if plan and plan.get("plan_start_scores", {}).get("strict") is not None:
        try:
            remaining = plan_aware_queue_count(state, plan)
        except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS:
            remaining = 0
        if remaining > 0:
            print_frozen_score_with_queue_context(plan, remaining)
            return

    # No active plan cycle or queue is clear — live scores
    from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.score_update import print_score_update

    print_score_update(state, prev)


__all__ = [
    "get_plan_start_strict",
    "plan_aware_queue_count",
    "print_execution_or_reveal",
    "print_frozen_score_with_queue_context",
]
```
```

"""Shared queue-construction policy for next/plan command surfaces."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from desloppify.core.enums import Status, canonical_status_filter
from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue


def build_plan_queue(
    state: dict[str, Any],
    *,
    tier: int | None = None,
    count: int | None = None,
    scope: str | None = None,
    status: str = Status.OPEN.value,
    target_strict: float = 95.0,
    no_tier_fallback: bool = False,
    explain: bool = False,
    plan: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
    include_skipped: bool = False,
    cluster: str | None = None,
    collapse_clusters: bool = False,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Build one queue with consistent policy defaults used by CLI surfaces."""
    normalized_status = canonical_status_filter(status, default=Status.OPEN.value)
    return build_work_queue(
        state,
        options=QueueBuildOptions(
            tier=tier,
            count=count,
            scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
            scope=scope,
            status=normalized_status,
            include_subjective=True,
            subjective_threshold=target_strict,
            no_tier_fallback=no_tier_fallback,
            explain=explain,
            plan=plan,
            include_skipped=include_skipped,
            cluster=cluster,
            collapse_clusters=collapse_clusters,
        ),
    )


__all__ = ["build_plan_queue"]
```

```
"""Typed finding/detail accessors for command-layer renderers."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Mapping, TypedDict, cast

from desloppify import state as state_mod


class FindingDetail(TypedDict, total=False):
    """Known finding detail keys used by command rendering paths."""

    category: str
    count: int
    dimension: str
    dimension_name: str
    importers: int
    investigation: bool
    line: int
    lines: list[int]
    strict_score: float
    suggestion: str


def state_findings(
    state: state_mod.StateModel | dict[str, Any],
    *,
    strict: bool = False,
) -> dict[str, state_mod.Finding]:
    """Return typed state findings map or raise on contract violations."""
    if strict:
        return state_mod.require_findings(state)
    findings = state.get("findings")
    if isinstance(findings, dict):
        return cast(dict[str, state_mod.Finding], findings)
    return {}


def finding_detail(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> FindingDetail:
    """Return typed detail mapping for a finding-like payload."""
    detail = finding.get("detail")
    if isinstance(detail, dict):
        return cast(FindingDetail, detail)
    return FindingDetail()


def detail_dimension(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> str:
    """Return normalized detail.dimension value."""
    return str(finding_detail(finding).get("dimension", "")).strip()


def detail_lines(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[int]:
    """Return normalized integer line list from finding detail."""
    raw_lines = finding_detail(finding).get("lines", [])
    if not isinstance(raw_lines, list):
        return []
    return [int(line) for line in raw_lines if isinstance(line, int | float)]


def detail_target_line(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> int | None:
    """Return primary target line from detail.line or detail.lines[0]."""
    detail = finding_detail(finding)
    line = detail.get("line")
    if isinstance(line, int):
        return line
    lines = detail_lines(finding)
    return lines[0] if lines else None


__all__ = [
    "FindingDetail",
    "detail_dimension",
    "detail_lines",
    "detail_target_line",
    "finding_detail",
    "state_findings",
]
```
```

"""Shared lazy-export helpers for command package ``__init__`` modules."""

from __future__ import annotations

import importlib
from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping, MutableMapping


def lazy_module_getattr(
    *,
    name: str,
    module_name: str,
    import_target: str,
    export_names: Iterable[str],
    namespace: MutableMapping[str, object],
) -> object:
    """Lazily import one exported attribute from a sibling command module."""
    allowed = set(export_names)
    if name not in allowed:
        raise AttributeError(f"module {module_name!r} has no attribute {name!r}")
    value = getattr(importlib.import_module(import_target, module_name), name)
    namespace[name] = value
    return value


def lazy_module_dir(
    *,
    namespace: Mapping[str, object],
    export_names: Iterable[str],
) -> list[str]:
    """Return module dir() entries including lazy exports."""
    return sorted(set(namespace) | set(export_names))
```

```
"""Stable review import mode normalization for command entrypoints."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Literal

ReviewImportMode = Literal[
    "auto",
    "trusted_internal",
    "attested_external",
    "manual_override",
]

_ALLOWED_MODES: set[str] = {
    "auto",
    "trusted_internal",
    "attested_external",
    "manual_override",
}


def normalize_review_import_mode(raw: str | None) -> ReviewImportMode:
    """Normalize user/caller import mode text to one stable mode token."""
    mode = (raw or "auto").strip().lower().replace("-", "_")
    if mode not in _ALLOWED_MODES:
        allowed = ", ".join(sorted(_ALLOWED_MODES))
        raise ValueError(f"invalid review import mode '{raw}'; expected one of: {allowed}")
    return mode  # type: ignore[return-value]


def _validate_mode_compatibility(
    *,
    mode: ReviewImportMode,
    attested: bool,
    manual: bool,
) -> None:
    if mode == "trusted_internal" and (attested or manual):
        raise ValueError(
            "import_mode=trusted_internal cannot be combined with attested/manual flags"
        )
    if mode == "attested_external" and manual:
        raise ValueError(
            "import_mode=attested_external cannot be combined with manual override flags"
        )
    if mode == "manual_override" and attested:
        raise ValueError(
            "import_mode=manual_override cannot be combined with attested external flags"
        )


def _apply_trusted_internal_defaults(
    trusted_source: bool,
    trusted_label: str | None,
) -> tuple[bool, str, bool, bool]:
    if not trusted_source:
        trusted_source = True
    if not isinstance(trusted_label, str) or not trusted_label.strip():
        trusted_label = "trusted internal run-batches import"
    return trusted_source, trusted_label, False, False


def apply_review_import_mode(
    *,
    import_mode: str | None,
    trusted_assessment_source: bool,
    trusted_assessment_label: str | None,
    attested_external: bool,
    manual_override: bool,
) -> tuple[bool, str | None, bool, bool]:
    """Apply a canonical import mode over legacy booleans without dropping compatibility."""
    mode = normalize_review_import_mode(import_mode)
    trusted_source = bool(trusted_assessment_source)
    trusted_label = trusted_assessment_label
    attested = bool(attested_external)
    manual = bool(manual_override)

    _validate_mode_compatibility(mode=mode, attested=attested, manual=manual)

    if mode == "trusted_internal":
        return _apply_trusted_internal_defaults(trusted_source, trusted_label)
    if mode == "attested_external":
        return trusted_source, trusted_label, True, False
    if mode == "manual_override":
        return trusted_source, trusted_label, False, True

    return trusted_source, trusted_label, attested, manual


__all__ = [
    "ReviewImportMode",
    "apply_review_import_mode",
    "normalize_review_import_mode",
]
```

```
"""Shared review packet preparation helpers used by prepare/batch flows."""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from desloppify.intelligence import review as review_mod

from .constants import DEFAULT_REVIEW_BATCH_MAX_FILES


def redacted_review_config(config: dict[str, object] | None) -> dict[str, object]:
    """Return review config payload with score-target hints removed."""
    if not isinstance(config, dict):
        return {}
    return {key: value for key, value in config.items() if key != "target_strict_score"}


def coerce_review_batch_file_limit(config: dict[str, object] | None) -> int | None:
    """Resolve per-batch review file cap from config (0/negative => unlimited)."""
    raw = (config or {}).get("review_batch_max_files", DEFAULT_REVIEW_BATCH_MAX_FILES)
    try:
        value = int(raw)
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return DEFAULT_REVIEW_BATCH_MAX_FILES
    return value if value > 0 else None


def build_run_batches_next_command(
    *,
    include_scan_after_import: bool,
    retrospective: bool,
    retrospective_max_issues: int,
    retrospective_max_batch_items: int,
) -> str:
    """Build canonical follow-up command for batch execution."""
    command = "desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel"
    if include_scan_after_import:
        command += " --scan-after-import"
    if retrospective:
        command += (
            " --retrospective"
            f" --retrospective-max-issues {retrospective_max_issues}"
            f" --retrospective-max-batch-items {retrospective_max_batch_items}"
        )
    return command


def build_review_packet(
    *,
    path: Path,
    lang_run,
    state: dict[str, Any],
    config: dict[str, object] | None,
    dimensions: list[str] | None,
    files: list[str] | None,
    narrative: dict[str, Any],
    retrospective: bool,
    retrospective_max_issues: int,
    retrospective_max_batch_items: int,
    include_scan_after_import: bool,
    prepare_holistic_review_fn=None,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Create the canonical holistic review packet payload."""
    prepare_fn = prepare_holistic_review_fn or review_mod.prepare_holistic_review
    packet = prepare_fn(
        path,
        lang_run,
        state,
        options=review_mod.HolisticReviewPrepareOptions(
            dimensions=dimensions,
            files=files or None,
            max_files_per_batch=coerce_review_batch_file_limit(config),
            include_issue_history=retrospective,
            issue_history_max_issues=retrospective_max_issues,
            issue_history_max_batch_items=retrospective_max_batch_items,
        ),
    )
    packet["config"] = redacted_review_config(config)
    packet["narrative"] = narrative
    packet["next_command"] = build_run_batches_next_command(
        include_scan_after_import=include_scan_after_import,
        retrospective=retrospective,
        retrospective_max_issues=retrospective_max_issues,
        retrospective_max_batch_items=retrospective_max_batch_items,
    )
    return packet


__all__ = [
    "build_review_packet",
    "build_run_batches_next_command",
    "coerce_review_batch_file_limit",
    "redacted_review_config",
]
```

```
"""Shared subjective-assessment integrity helpers for review import flows."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Callable


def subjective_at_target_dimensions(
    state_or_dim_scores: dict,
    dim_scores: dict | None = None,
    *,
    target: float,
    scorecard_subjective_entries_fn,
    matches_target_score_fn,
) -> list[dict]:
    """Return scorecard-aligned subjective rows that sit on the target threshold."""
    state = state_or_dim_scores
    if dim_scores is None:
        dim_scores = state_or_dim_scores
        state = {"dimension_scores": dim_scores}

    rows: list[dict] = []
    for entry in scorecard_subjective_entries_fn(state, dim_scores=dim_scores):
        if entry.get("placeholder"):
            continue
        strict_val = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
        if matches_target_score_fn(strict_val, target):
            rows.append(
                {
                    "name": str(entry.get("name", "Subjective")),
                    "score": strict_val,
                    "cli_keys": list(entry.get("cli_keys", [])),
                }
            )
    rows.sort(key=lambda item: item["name"].lower())
    return rows


def bind_scorecard_subjective_at_target(
    *,
    scorecard_subjective_entries_fn=None,
    reporting_dimensions_mod=None,
    subjective_integrity_mod,
) -> Callable[..., list[dict]]:
    """Bind scorecard-specific dependencies once for import/output call sites.

    ``reporting_dimensions_mod`` is kept for compatibility; new callers should
    pass ``scorecard_subjective_entries_fn`` explicitly.
    """
    if scorecard_subjective_entries_fn is None and reporting_dimensions_mod is not None:
        scorecard_subjective_entries_fn = (
            reporting_dimensions_mod.scorecard_subjective_entries
        )
    if not callable(scorecard_subjective_entries_fn):
        raise ValueError("scorecard_subjective_entries_fn must be callable")

    def bound(
        state_or_dim_scores: dict[str, object],
        dim_scores: dict[str, object] | None = None,
        *,
        target: float,
    ) -> list[dict]:
        return subjective_at_target_dimensions(
            state_or_dim_scores,
            dim_scores,
            target=target,
            scorecard_subjective_entries_fn=scorecard_subjective_entries_fn,
            matches_target_score_fn=subjective_integrity_mod.matches_target_score,
        )

    return bound


__all__ = [
    "bind_scorecard_subjective_at_target",
    "subjective_at_target_dimensions",
]
```

```
"""Agent-environment detection helpers for scan reporting."""

from __future__ import annotations

import os


def is_agent_environment() -> bool:
    """Return True when scan is running inside a supported AI agent runtime."""
    return bool(
        os.environ.get("CLAUDE_CODE")
        or os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_AGENT")
        or os.environ.get("GEMINI_CLI")
        or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED")
        or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX")
        or os.environ.get("CURSOR_TRACE_ID")
    )


def detect_agent_interface() -> str | None:
    """Resolve current interface key used by `update-skill`."""
    if os.environ.get("CLAUDE_CODE"):
        return "claude"
    if os.environ.get("GEMINI_CLI"):
        return "gemini"
    if os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED") or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX"):
        return "codex"
    if os.environ.get("CURSOR_TRACE_ID"):
        return "cursor"
    return None


__all__ = ["detect_agent_interface", "is_agent_environment"]
```

```
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..4164597
--- /dev/null

@@ -0,0 +1,88 @@
+"""Plan-aware queue progress and frozen score display helpers."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+from desloppify.core.exception_sets import PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS
+from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
+
+
+def plan_aware_queue_count(state: dict, plan: dict | None = None) -> int:
+    """Count remaining plan-aware queue items (skips excluded, clusters collapsed)."""
+    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue
+
+    result = build_work_queue(
+        state,
+        options=QueueBuildOptions(
+            status="open",
+            count=None,
+            plan=plan,
+            collapse_clusters=True,
+            include_skipped=False,
+        ),
+    )
+    return result["total"]
+
+
+def get_plan_start_strict(plan: dict | None) -> float | None:
+    """Extract the frozen plan-start strict score, or None if unset."""
+    if not plan:
+        return None
+    return plan.get("plan_start_scores", {}).get("strict")
+
+
+def print_frozen_score_with_queue_context(
+    plan: dict,
+    queue_remaining: int,
+) -> None:
+    """Show frozen plan-start score + queue progress."""
+    scores = plan.get("plan_start_scores", {})
+    strict = scores.get("strict")
+    if strict is None:
+        return
+    print(
+        colorize(
+            f"\n  Score (frozen at plan start): strict {strict:.1f}/100",
+            "cyan",
+        )
+    )
+    print(
+        colorize(
+            f"  Queue: {queue_remaining} item{'s' if queue_remaining != 1 else ''}"
+            " remaining. Score will not update until the queue is clear and you run `desloppify scan`.",
+            "dim",
+        )
+    )
+
+
+def print_execution_or_reveal(
+    state: dict,
+    prev,
+    plan: dict | None,
+) -> None:
+    """Context-aware score display: frozen plan-start score or live scores.
+
+    If a plan has ``plan_start_scores`` and there are queue items remaining,
+    show the frozen score + queue progress.  Otherwise delegate to the
+    existing live ``print_score_update``.
+    """
+    if plan and plan.get("plan_start_scores", {}).get("strict") is not None:
+        try:
+            remaining = plan_aware_queue_count(state, plan)
+        except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS:
+            remaining = 0
+        if remaining > 0:
+            print_frozen_score_with_queue_context(plan, remaining)
+            return
+
+    # No active plan cycle or queue is clear — live scores
+    from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.score_update import print_score_update
+
+    print_score_update(state, prev)
+
+
+__all__ = [
+    "get_plan_start_strict",
+    "plan_aware_queue_count",
+    "print_execution_or_reveal",
+    "print_frozen_score_with_queue_context",
+]
```

Here is the full output and analysis.

---

## File 1: `desloppify/app/commands/next.py`

### Full diff output

(Shown above in the bash output.)

### Analysis: New functionality vs. reorganization

**Pure reorganization (no behavior change):**

1. **Import style change** -- `from desloppify.app.commands.next_parts import output as next_output_mod` changed to `import desloppify.app.commands.next_parts.output as next_output_mod`. Same module, different import syntax. No effect.

2. **Hoisting inline imports to top-level** -- `from desloppify.engine.plan import load_plan`, `from desloppify.scoring import merge_potentials` moved from inside `_get_items()` to module-level. No behavior change.

3. **Extracting `_resolve_cluster_focus()`** -- The 4 lines of cluster-focus resolution logic were pulled out of `_get_items()` into a standalone function. Identical logic.

4. **Extracting `_build_next_payload()`** -- The payload construction block (calling `build_query_payload`, adding `overall_score`, `objective_score`, `strict_score`, `scorecard_dimensions`, `subjective_measures`) was pulled into a helper. Identical logic.

5. **Extracting `_emit_requested_output()`** -- The output-file and non-terminal-output branching was pulled into a helper. Identical logic.

6. **Extracting `_merge_potentials_safe()`** -- The `try/except` around `merge_potentials` was pulled into a helper. Identical logic.

**Genuinely new functionality:**

1. **New import and use of `queue_progress` helpers** -- Imports `get_plan_start_strict` and `plan_aware_queue_count` from a new module `desloppify/app/commands/helpers/queue_progress.py`. These are called in the new `_plan_queue_context()` helper.

2. **New `_plan_queue_context()` function** -- This is entirely new logic. It computes two values: `plan_start_strict` (the frozen strict score at plan creation time) and `queue_total` (how many items remain in the plan-aware queue). These are used to give the user lifecycle/progress context.

3. **New import of `PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS`** from `desloppify.core.exception_sets` -- Used inside `_plan_queue_context()` to catch plan-loading errors gracefully.

4. **Passing `plan_start_strict` and `target_strict` to `show_empty_queue()`** -- New keyword arguments forwarded to `render.show_empty_queue()`, enabling the new "queue cleared" display (see render.py analysis below).

5. **Passing `cluster_filter=effective_cluster` to `render_terminal_items()`** -- New keyword argument that enables the compact cluster drill-in rendering (see render.py analysis below).

6. **Passing `queue_total` and `plan_start_strict` to `render_followup_nudges()`** -- New keyword arguments enabling the plan-lifecycle progress display and rescan nudge (see render.py analysis below).

---

## File 2: `desloppify/app/commands/next_parts/__init__.py`

### Full diff output

The entire `__init__.py` was gutted from a 35-line file with explicit re-exports and `__all__` down to a single docstring:

```python
"""Helpers used by the `next` command implementation."""
```

### Analysis

**Pure reorganization.** The old file re-exported everything from `output` and `render` as a convenience. The abandoned-refactor branch removed all those re-exports because `next.py` now imports the submodules directly (`import desloppify.app.commands.next_parts.output as next_output_mod`). No new functionality -- just removing the re-export facade.

**Potential risk:** Any external code that did `from desloppify.app.commands.next_parts import render_terminal_items` would break. But within the codebase, `next.py` is the only consumer and it was updated to use direct module imports.

---

## File 3: `desloppify/app/commands/next_parts/render.py`

### Full diff output

(Shown above in the bash output.)

### Analysis: New functionality vs. reorganization

**Pure reorganization (no behavior change):**

1. **Hoisting `from collections import Counter`** to module-level from inside `_render_cluster_item()`. No behavior change.

2. **Hoisting `from desloppify.scoring import compute_health_breakdown, compute_score_impact`** to module-level from inline imports inside `_render_item()`. No behavior change.

3. **Changing `except ... : pass` to `except ... as exc: _ = exc`** in two places inside `_render_item()`. This is a lint/style change (likely to satisfy a "bare except pass" linter rule). No behavior change.

**Genuinely new functionality:**

1. **Changed resolution hint command format (two places):**
   - In `_render_cluster_item()`: `--note "<what>" --attest "{ATTEST_EXAMPLE}"` changed to `--note "<what>" --confirm`
   - In `render_single_item_resolution_hint()`: same change, `--attest "{ATTEST_EXAMPLE}"` replaced with `--confirm`
   
   This is a **user-facing behavior change** -- the suggested command to resolve items now uses `--confirm` instead of `--attest`. This implies the `plan done` command's interface changed (or will change) to use `--confirm` instead of requiring an attestation string.

2. **Enhanced `show_empty_queue()` with plan lifecycle display:**
   - New parameters: `plan_start_strict: float | None = None`, `target_strict: float | None = None`
   - When `plan_start_strict` is available and the queue is empty, it now shows a before/after comparison: `"Frozen plan-start: strict X → Live estimate: strict Y (+delta)"` with a nudge to run `desloppify scan` to finalize the score. This replaces the old simple `"Nothing to do! Strict score: X/100"` message when plan context exists.
   - The old behavior is preserved as the `else` branch when plan context is not available.

3. **New `_render_compact_item()` function:**
   - Entirely new. Renders a one-line summary for cluster drill-in items (after the first item). Shows tier, effort tag, summary, file, and a short ID.

4. **New cluster drill-in rendering in `render_terminal_items()`:**
   - New parameter: `cluster_filter: str | None = None`
   - New logic: when there are multiple items AND either `cluster_filter` is set or the plan has an `active_cluster`, it activates "cluster drill-in" mode. The first item gets the full rendering card, but all subsequent items get the compact one-line `_render_compact_item()` treatment. This is a significant UX change for cluster-focused workflows.

5. **Enhanced `render_followup_nudges()` with plan-cycle progress:**
   - New parameters: `queue_total: int = 0`, `plan_start_strict: float | None = None`
   - **New plan-cycle progress block:** When `queue_total > 0` and `plan_start_strict` is available, it shows the frozen plan-start score and remaining queue count, with a note that the score won't update until the queue clears and you rescan. This takes priority over the old gap/target display (which becomes the `elif` branch).

6. **New subjective bottleneck banner:**
   - Entirely new block. After the strict-score/target display, if `strict_score` and `dim_scores` are available, it calls `compute_health_breakdown()` and compares subjective drag vs mechanical drag. If subjective drag exceeds mechanical drag AND is greater than 5.0 points, it prints a prominent yellow banner telling the user that subjective dimensions are the main bottleneck and suggests running `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.

7. **New rescan nudge after structural work:**
   - Entirely new block at the bottom of `render_followup_nudges()`. When `queue_total > 10`, it prints a tip suggesting the user rescan after structural fixes to let cascade effects settle.

---

## Summary of genuinely new behaviors

| Feature | Location | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Plan-cycle progress tracking | `next.py` `_plan_queue_context()` | Computes frozen plan-start score and remaining queue count |
| "Queue cleared" celebration | `render.py` `show_empty_queue()` | Before/after score comparison when queue empties |
| Cluster drill-in compact view | `render.py` `_render_compact_item()` + `render_terminal_items()` | First item full card, rest compact one-liners |
| Plan-cycle progress footer | `render.py` `render_followup_nudges()` | Shows frozen score + queue remaining during active cycle |
| Subjective bottleneck banner | `render.py` `render_followup_nudges()` | Yellow warning when subjective drag > mechanical drag |
| Rescan nudge for large queues | `render.py` `render_followup_nudges()` | Tip to rescan after structural work when queue > 10 |
| `--confirm` replaces `--attest` | `render.py` (two places) | Resolution hint commands changed |

Everything else (5 extracted helpers in `next.py`, hoisted imports, `__init__.py` gutting, `except` style changes) is pure reorganization with no behavioral impact.

## 2. `desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py`

**Full diff shown above.**

**NEW functionality:**

1. **Plan-aware "frozen score" display replaces live score movement.** This is the single biggest functional change. On `main`, after resolving, `cmd_resolve` calls `_print_score_movement(...)` which always shows live before/after score deltas. On `abandoned-refactor`, it calls `print_execution_or_reveal(state, prev, _resolve_plan)` instead. This new function (from `queue_progress.py`) implements **entirely new behavior**: if a living plan exists with `plan_start_scores` and the work queue still has items, it shows a **frozen plan-start score + queue progress count** instead of live score deltas. Only when the queue is empty does it fall back to the old live `print_score_update`. This means users mid-plan will see "Score (frozen at plan start): strict X/100" plus "Queue: N items remaining" instead of per-resolve score changes.

2. **`cmd_ignore_pattern` also gets plan-aware display.** On `main`, `cmd_ignore_pattern` calls `print_score_update(state, prev)`. On `abandoned-refactor`, it calls `print_execution_or_reveal(state, prev, _ignore_plan)`, gaining the same frozen-score-during-plan behavior. This is genuinely new -- ignore-pattern previously never interacted with the living plan for display purposes.

3. **Imports moved from inline to top-level.** On `main`, `from desloppify.engine.plan import has_living_plan, load_plan, purge_ids, save_plan` was inside the `try` block in `cmd_resolve`. On `abandoned-refactor`, it's a top-level import. Similarly for the config and score helpers that were previously imported inline in `_print_score_movement` logic. This is a minor structural change (module loaded at import time vs. lazily), not new functionality per se, but it does change failure modes -- if `engine.plan` can't be imported, the entire module fails to load now rather than gracefully catching the error.

4. **Removal of the inline `has_review_findings` + `target_strict` + `_print_score_movement` block.** On `main`, `cmd_resolve` computed `has_review_findings` (checking if any resolved finding had detector == "review"), loaded config, computed `target_strict`, and called `_print_score_movement` with all of those. On `abandoned-refactor`, all of that is replaced by `print_execution_or_reveal`. The review-findings-specific guidance ("Scores unchanged -- review findings don't affect scores directly") is **still present** in `render.py`'s `_print_post_resolve_guidance`, but it is **no longer called from the main resolve path in cmd.py**. The `_print_score_movement` function still exists in `render.py` but is no longer imported into `cmd.py`. This means the detailed per-resolve score movement with wontfix gap warnings, review-finding notes, and strict-target nudges is **lost from the primary resolve flow** and replaced by the simpler frozen-or-live display.

## Summary of Genuinely New Functionality

There is exactly **one new feature** across all four files, and it lives in `cmd.py`'s switch from `_print_score_movement` to `print_execution_or_reveal`:

**Plan-aware frozen score display during active plan execution.** When a living plan has `plan_start_scores` set and the work queue still has remaining items, both `cmd_resolve` and `cmd_ignore_pattern` now show a frozen "score at plan start" plus queue progress count, instead of live before/after score deltas. This is backed by the entirely new `desloppify/app/commands/helpers/queue_progress.py` module which provides:
- `plan_aware_queue_count()` -- counts remaining queue items with cluster collapsing
- `get_plan_start_strict()` -- extracts frozen strict score from plan
- `print_frozen_score_with_queue_context()` -- renders the frozen score + queue count
- `print_execution_or_reveal()` -- the dispatch function that chooses frozen vs. live display

A **secondary consequence** is that the detailed resolve-specific score movement output (wontfix strict-gap warnings, review-finding guidance, strict-target nudges) is no longer shown in the primary resolve flow -- it's replaced by the simpler frozen-or-live display. The `_print_score_movement` function and its extracted helpers still exist in `render.py` but are now **dead code** from the perspective of `cmd.py` (no longer imported there). This is a functional regression or intentional simplification depending on intent.

There's also a **small new behavior in `score_update.py`** (not one of the four files but a changed dependency): a new "Score dropped" reassurance message when `new.strict < prev.strict`, telling users score drops are normal after structural fixes.

## 3. `runner_helpers.py`

**Full diff** (shown above)

**New functionality:**
- **Narrowed exception handling in task execution and callbacks.** On `main`, `_emit_progress` catches bare `Exception`, `_execute_serial` catches bare `Exception`, `_execute_parallel` catches bare `Exception`, and `_heartbeat` catches bare `Exception`. On the branch, these are replaced with specific tuples `_RUNNER_CALLBACK_EXCEPTIONS` and `_RUNNER_TASK_EXCEPTIONS`. This means exceptions *not* in those tuples (e.g., `KeyboardInterrupt`, `SystemExit`, or unexpected types like `AttributeError`, `IndexError`, `StopIteration`) will now propagate instead of being silently swallowed. This is a genuine behavioral change -- it tightens error handling to avoid masking bugs.
- **Guard for empty `failures` list in `print_failures_and_exit`.** New `if not failures:` block prints `"Failed batches: []"` to stderr before continuing to `_print_failures_report`. On `main`, this would pass an empty list into the report with no special handling.
- **`_runner_failure_hints` / `_any_restricted_sandbox_failures` structural change.** The `try/except OSError` that previously used `continue` on failure now initializes `raw = ""` / `text = ""` before the try, catches the exception, and uses `if not raw: continue` / `if not text: continue` after. This is subtly different: on `main`, if `read_text()` raises `OSError` the `continue` skips directly; on the branch, any `OSError` is caught, but the `continue` only happens if the result is empty string. The net effect is the same for `OSError`, but the variable is now always defined.

**Pure refactoring / cosmetic:**
- `_ = exc` pattern replacing bare `pass` in exception handlers throughout.
- Defining `_RUNNER_CALLBACK_EXCEPTIONS` and `_RUNNER_TASK_EXCEPTIONS` as named tuples at module level (structure change, but the *choice of which exceptions* is the behavioral part).

## 1. `finding_payloads.py` -- Typed finding/detail accessors

```python
"""Typed finding/detail accessors for command-layer renderers."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Mapping, TypedDict, cast

from desloppify import state as state_mod


class FindingDetail(TypedDict, total=False):
    """Known finding detail keys used by command rendering paths."""

    category: str
    count: int
    dimension: str
    dimension_name: str
    importers: int
    investigation: bool
    line: int
    lines: list[int]
    strict_score: float
    suggestion: str


def state_findings(
    state: state_mod.StateModel | dict[str, Any],
    *,
    strict: bool = False,
) -> dict[str, state_mod.Finding]:
    """Return typed state findings map or raise on contract violations."""
    if strict:
        return state_mod.require_findings(state)
    findings = state.get("findings")
    if isinstance(findings, dict):
        return cast(dict[str, state_mod.Finding], findings)
    return {}


def finding_detail(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> FindingDetail:
    """Return typed detail mapping for a finding-like payload."""
    detail = finding.get("detail")
    if isinstance(detail, dict):
        return cast(FindingDetail, detail)
    return FindingDetail()


def detail_dimension(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> str:
    """Return normalized detail.dimension value."""
    return str(finding_detail(finding).get("dimension", "")).strip()


def detail_lines(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[int]:
    """Return normalized integer line list from finding detail."""
    raw_lines = finding_detail(finding).get("lines", [])
    if not isinstance(raw_lines, list):
        return []
    return [int(line) for line in raw_lines if isinstance(line, int | float)]


def detail_target_line(finding: Mapping[str, Any]) -> int | None:
    """Return primary target line from detail.line or detail.lines[0]."""
    detail = finding_detail(finding)
    line = detail.get("line")
    if isinstance(line, int):
        return line
    lines = detail_lines(finding)
    return lines[0] if lines else None


__all__ = [
    "FindingDetail",
    "detail_dimension",
    "detail_lines",
    "detail_target_line",
    "finding_detail",
    "state_findings",
]
```

**Verdict: Pure refactoring / type-safety layer.** No new behavior. This is a `TypedDict` + typed accessor wrappers around dictionary `.get()` calls that already exist elsewhere. The functions (`state_findings`, `finding_detail`, `detail_dimension`, `detail_lines`, `detail_target_line`) are all defensive wrappers over existing data patterns. No new features.

---

## 2. `lazy_exports.py` -- Lazy module import helpers

```python
"""Shared lazy-export helpers for command package ``__init__`` modules."""

from __future__ import annotations

import importlib
from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping, MutableMapping


def lazy_module_getattr(
    *,
    name: str,
    module_name: str,
    import_target: str,
    export_names: Iterable[str],
    namespace: MutableMapping[str, object],
) -> object:
    """Lazily import one exported attribute from a sibling command module."""
    allowed = set(export_names)
    if name not in allowed:
        raise AttributeError(f"module {module_name!r} has no attribute {name!r}")
    value = getattr(importlib.import_module(import_target, module_name), name)
    namespace[name] = value
    return value


def lazy_module_dir(
    *,
    namespace: Mapping[str, object],
    export_names: Iterable[str],
) -> list[str]:
    """Return module dir() entries including lazy exports."""
    return sorted(set(namespace) | set(export_names))
```

**Verdict: Pure infrastructure refactoring.** Generic lazy-import machinery for `__init__.py` modules. No user-facing behavior or features whatsoever.

---

## 3. `importing/modes.py` -- Review import mode normalization

```python
"""Stable review import mode normalization for command entrypoints."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Literal

ReviewImportMode = Literal[
    "auto",
    "trusted_internal",
    "attested_external",
    "manual_override",
]

_ALLOWED_MODES: set[str] = {
    "auto",
    "trusted_internal",
    "attested_external",
    "manual_override",
}


def normalize_review_import_mode(raw: str | None) -> ReviewImportMode:
    """Normalize user/caller import mode text to one stable mode token."""
    mode = (raw or "auto").strip().lower().replace("-", "_")
    if mode not in _ALLOWED_MODES:
        allowed = ", ".join(sorted(_ALLOWED_MODES))
        raise ValueError(f"invalid review import mode '{raw}'; expected one of: {allowed}")
    return mode  # type: ignore[return-value]


def _validate_mode_compatibility(
    *,
    mode: ReviewImportMode,
    attested: bool,
    manual: bool,
) -> None:
    if mode == "trusted_internal" and (attested or manual):
        raise ValueError(
            "import_mode=trusted_internal cannot be combined with attested/manual flags"
        )
    if mode == "attested_external" and manual:
        raise ValueError(
            "import_mode=attested_external cannot be combined with manual override flags"
        )
    if mode == "manual_override" and attested:
        raise ValueError(
            "import_mode=manual_override cannot be combined with attested external flags"
        )


def _apply_trusted_internal_defaults(
    trusted_source: bool,
    trusted_label: str | None,
) -> tuple[bool, str, bool, bool]:
    if not trusted_source:
        trusted_source = True
    if not isinstance(trusted_label, str) or not trusted_label.strip():
        trusted_label = "trusted internal run-batches import"
    return trusted_source, trusted_label, False, False


def apply_review_import_mode(
    *,
    import_mode: str | None,
    trusted_assessment_source: bool,
    trusted_assessment_label: str | None,
    attested_external: bool,
    manual_override: bool,
) -> tuple[bool, str | None, bool, bool]:
    """Apply a canonical import mode over legacy booleans without dropping compatibility."""
    mode = normalize_review_import_mode(import_mode)
    trusted_source = bool(trusted_assessment_source)
    trusted_label = trusted_assessment_label
    attested = bool(attested_external)
    manual = bool(manual_override)

    _validate_mode_compatibility(mode=mode, attested=attested, manual=manual)

    if mode == "trusted_internal":
        return _apply_trusted_internal_defaults(trusted_source, trusted_label)
    if mode == "attested_external":
        return trusted_source, trusted_label, True, False
    if mode == "manual_override":
        return trusted_source, trusted_label, False, True

    return trusted_source, trusted_label, attested, manual


__all__ = [
    "ReviewImportMode",
    "apply_review_import_mode",
    "normalize_review_import_mode",
]
```

**Verdict: Genuinely new feature -- review import mode system.** This introduces a formal `ReviewImportMode` type (`auto`, `trusted_internal`, `attested_external`, `manual_override`) with:
- Input normalization (case/hyphen handling, validation)
- Mutual exclusion validation (e.g., `trusted_internal` cannot combine with attested/manual flags)
- Default application logic (`trusted_internal` auto-sets `trusted_source=True` with a default label)
- A clean `apply_review_import_mode()` function that replaces ad-hoc boolean-flag handling with a single mode-based dispatch

This is a real behavioral addition -- it formalizes what was previously a set of loose boolean flags into a validated, typed mode system.

---

## 4. `payload/constants.py` -- Review batch constant

```python
"""Shared constants for review command flows."""

from __future__ import annotations

DEFAULT_REVIEW_BATCH_MAX_FILES = 80
```

## 5. `payload/packet_builder.py` -- Review packet preparation helpers

```python
"""Shared review packet preparation helpers used by prepare/batch flows."""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from desloppify.intelligence import review as review_mod

from .constants import DEFAULT_REVIEW_BATCH_MAX_FILES


def redacted_review_config(config: dict[str, object] | None) -> dict[str, object]:
    """Return review config payload with score-target hints removed."""
    if not isinstance(config, dict):
        return {}
    return {key: value for key, value in config.items() if key != "target_strict_score"}


def coerce_review_batch_file_limit(config: dict[str, object] | None) -> int | None:
    """Resolve per-batch review file cap from config (0/negative => unlimited)."""
    raw = (config or {}).get("review_batch_max_files", DEFAULT_REVIEW_BATCH_MAX_FILES)
    try:
        value = int(raw)
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return DEFAULT_REVIEW_BATCH_MAX_FILES
    return value if value > 0 else None


def build_run_batches_next_command(
    *,
    include_scan_after_import: bool,
    retrospective: bool,
    retrospective_max_issues: int,
    retrospective_max_batch_items: int,
) -> str:
    """Build canonical follow-up command for batch execution."""
    command = "desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel"
    if include_scan_after_import:
        command += " --scan-after-import"
    if retrospective:
        command += (
            " --retrospective"
            f" --retrospective-max-issues {retrospective_max_issues}"
            f" --retrospective-max-batch-items {retrospective_max_batch_items}"
        )
    return command


def build_review_packet(
    *,
    path: Path,
    lang_run,
    state: dict[str, Any],
    config: dict[str, object] | None,
    dimensions: list[str] | None,
    files: list[str] | None,
    narrative: dict[str, Any],
    retrospective: bool,
    retrospective_max_issues: int,
    retrospective_max_batch_items: int,
    include_scan_after_import: bool,
    prepare_holistic_review_fn=None,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Create the canonical holistic review packet payload."""
    prepare_fn = prepare_holistic_review_fn or review_mod.prepare_holistic_review
    packet = prepare_fn(
        path,
        lang_run,
        state,
        options=review_mod.HolisticReviewPrepareOptions(
            dimensions=dimensions,
            files=files or None,
            max_files_per_batch=coerce_review_batch_file_limit(config),
            include_issue_history=retrospective,
            issue_history_max_issues=retrospective_max_issues,
            issue_history_max_batch_items=retrospective_max_batch_items,
        ),
    )
    packet["config"] = redacted_review_config(config)
    packet["narrative"] = narrative
    packet["next_command"] = build_run_batches_next_command(
        include_scan_after_import=include_scan_after_import,
        retrospective=retrospective,
        retrospective_max_issues=retrospective_max_issues,
        retrospective_max_batch_items=retrospective_max_batch_items,
    )
    return packet


__all__ = [
    "build_review_packet",
    "build_run_batches_next_command",
    "coerce_review_batch_file_limit",
    "redacted_review_config",
]
```

**Verdict: Mostly refactoring with one minor new behavior.** The `coerce_review_batch_file_limit` function adds new behavior: it reads a `review_batch_max_files` config key, coerces it to int, and treats 0/negative as "unlimited" (returns `None`). This is a new config-driven batch file limit feature. The `redacted_review_config` stripping `target_strict_score` from the packet payload is also a new defensive behavior (prevents score-gaming hints from leaking into review packets). The rest (`build_review_packet`, `build_run_batches_next_command`) is extraction of existing logic.

## 6. `quality/assessment_integrity.py` -- Subjective assessment integrity

```python
"""Shared subjective-assessment integrity helpers for review import flows."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Callable


def subjective_at_target_dimensions(
    state_or_dim_scores: dict,
    dim_scores: dict | None = None,
    *,
    target: float,
    scorecard_subjective_entries_fn,
    matches_target_score_fn,
) -> list[dict]:
    """Return scorecard-aligned subjective rows that sit on the target threshold."""
    state = state_or_dim_scores
    if dim_scores is None:
        dim_scores = state_or_dim_scores
        state = {"dimension_scores": dim_scores}

    rows: list[dict] = []
    for entry in scorecard_subjective_entries_fn(state, dim_scores=dim_scores):
        if entry.get("placeholder"):
            continue
        strict_val = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
        if matches_target_score_fn(strict_val, target):
            rows.append(
                {
                    "name": str(entry.get("name", "Subjective")),
                    "score": strict_val,
                    "cli_keys": list(entry.get("cli_keys", [])),
                }
            )
    rows.sort(key=lambda item: item["name"].lower())
    return rows


def bind_scorecard_subjective_at_target(
    *,
    scorecard_subjective_entries_fn=None,
    reporting_dimensions_mod=None,
    subjective_integrity_mod,
) -> Callable[..., list[dict]]:
    """Bind scorecard-specific dependencies once for import/output call sites."""
    if scorecard_subjective_entries_fn is None and reporting_dimensions_mod is not None:
        scorecard_subjective_entries_fn = (
            reporting_dimensions_mod.scorecard_subjective_entries
        )
    if not callable(scorecard_subjective_entries_fn):
        raise ValueError("scorecard_subjective_entries_fn must be callable")

    def bound(
        state_or_dim_scores: dict[str, object],
        dim_scores: dict[str, object] | None = None,
        *,
        target: float,
    ) -> list[dict]:
        return subjective_at_target_dimensions(
            state_or_dim_scores,
            dim_scores,
            target=target,
            scorecard_subjective_entries_fn=scorecard_subjective_entries_fn,
            matches_target_score_fn=subjective_integrity_mod.matches_target_score,
        )

    return bound


__all__ = [
    "bind_scorecard_subjective_at_target",
    "subjective_at_target_dimensions",
]
```

**Verdict: Likely refactoring extraction, but need to check.** The `subjective_at_target_dimensions` function identifies subjective dimensions that are sitting exactly on the target score threshold -- this is an integrity check for review import. The `bind_scorecard_subjective_at_target` is a dependency-injection wrapper. This looks like it was extracted from inline code rather than being net-new logic. No obviously new features, but the extraction does make the "at-target subjective dimension" check reusable across call sites.

## 8. `scan_agent_env.py` -- Agent environment detection

```python
"""Agent-environment detection helpers for scan reporting."""

from __future__ import annotations

import os


def is_agent_environment() -> bool:
    """Return True when scan is running inside a supported AI agent runtime."""
    return bool(
        os.environ.get("CLAUDE_CODE")
        or os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_AGENT")
        or os.environ.get("GEMINI_CLI")
        or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED")
        or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX")
        or os.environ.get("CURSOR_TRACE_ID")
    )


def detect_agent_interface() -> str | None:
    """Resolve current interface key used by `update-skill`."""
    if os.environ.get("CLAUDE_CODE"):
        return "claude"
    if os.environ.get("GEMINI_CLI"):
        return "gemini"
    if os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX_NETWORK_DISABLED") or os.environ.get("CODEX_SANDBOX"):
        return "codex"
    if os.environ.get("CURSOR_TRACE_ID"):
        return "cursor"
    return None


__all__ = ["detect_agent_interface", "is_agent_environment"]
```

## 9. `score_update.py` diff -- Score-drop reassurance message

The diff shows:
- Import reordering (moving lazy imports to top-level -- pure refactoring)
- **NEW BEHAVIOR: Score-drop reassurance message.** When `new.strict < prev.strict`, a yellow message is printed: `"Score dropped -- this is normal after structural fixes. New findings may surface; keep working the queue."`

**Verdict: One genuinely new behavior.** The score-drop reassurance message is a new UX feature that provides coaching when a user's strict score decreases.

---

## 10. `queue_progress.py` diff -- Plan-aware queue progress + frozen score display (entirely new file)

This is a completely new file with these functions:

- `plan_aware_queue_count(state, plan)` -- Counts remaining queue items with plan awareness (clusters collapsed, skipped excluded)
- `get_plan_start_strict(plan)` -- Extracts the frozen "plan start" strict score from plan data
- `print_frozen_score_with_queue_context(plan, queue_remaining)` -- Displays the frozen plan-start score and remaining queue count, with messaging that score won't update until queue is clear + re-scan
- `print_execution_or_reveal(state, prev, plan)` -- Context-aware dispatch: if there's an active plan cycle with queue items remaining, shows frozen score; otherwise falls through to live `print_score_update`

**Verdict: Genuinely new feature -- frozen plan-start score display.** This implements a "score freeze" UX during plan execution. When a plan is active and has `plan_start_scores`, the user sees the frozen score from when they started the plan, plus a count of remaining queue items and a message that the score won't update until the queue is cleared. This prevents confusing score fluctuations during active fix work.

---

## 11. `queue_render.py` diff -- Plan queue rendering

The diff refactors `cmd_plan_queue()` by extracting helper functions:
- `_resolve_plan_context()` -- Extracts plan data and effective cluster resolution
- `_print_queue_header()` -- Extracts header printing logic
- `_queue_display_items()` -- Extracts top-N slicing
- `_build_rows()` -- Extracts row building

The logic is identical; one minor cleanup: `suffix = " [skip]" if item.get("plan_skipped") else ""` replaces a multi-line version. The skipped-items hint now uses an em-dash instead of a unicode character.

**Verdict: Pure refactoring.** No new behavior at all.

---

## 12. `override_handlers.py` diff -- Plan override commands

The diff shows:
- Moving lazy imports to top-level (pure refactoring)
- **NEW BEHAVIOR: `--confirm` flag on `cmd_plan_done`.** When `args.confirm` is True and `--note` is provided, it auto-generates an attestation string: `"I have actually {note} and I am not gaming the score."`. If `--confirm` is used without `--note`, it prints an error. This is a UX shortcut so users don't have to manually type attestation text.

**Verdict: One genuinely new feature -- `--confirm` flag for `plan done`.** This adds a convenience flag that auto-generates the attestation from a note, reducing friction when marking findings as fixed.

---

## 13. `status_cmd.py` diff -- Frozen score display in status

The diff integrates the `queue_progress` module into the status command:
- Imports `get_plan_start_strict`, `plan_aware_queue_count`, `print_frozen_score_with_queue_context`
- When there is an active plan with `plan_start_scores` and remaining queue items, it shows the frozen plan-start score instead of live scores
- Falls through to the existing score summary lines when no active plan cycle exists

**Verdict: Genuinely new behavior -- same frozen-score feature surfaced in `status`.** This is the consumer side of `queue_progress.py`, making the `status` command plan-aware.

---

## Summary: What is genuinely new

| File | New Behavior? | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `finding_payloads.py` | No | TypedDict + typed accessors, pure refactoring |
| `lazy_exports.py` | No | Lazy import infrastructure |
| `importing/modes.py` | **Yes** | Formal `ReviewImportMode` system replacing ad-hoc boolean flags with validated modes (`auto`, `trusted_internal`, `attested_external`, `manual_override`) |
| `payload/constants.py` | No | Extracted constant |
| `payload/packet_builder.py` | **Minor** | New `review_batch_max_files` config key support (0/negative = unlimited), and `target_strict_score` redaction from review packets |
| `quality/assessment_integrity.py` | No | Extracted/reusable subjective-at-target check |
| `orchestration/runtime_defaults.py` | No | Extracted constant |
| `scan_agent_env.py` | No | Extracted existing env detection |
| `score_update.py` | **Yes** | Score-drop reassurance message when strict score decreases |
| `queue_progress.py` | **Yes** | Frozen plan-start score display during active queue cycles (new UX feature) |
| `queue_render.py` | No | Pure refactoring of queue rendering |
| `override_handlers.py` | **Yes** | `--confirm` flag on `plan done` that auto-generates attestation from `--note` |
| `status_cmd.py` | **Yes** | Integrates frozen plan-start score into the `status` command |

The four genuinely new features are:

1. **Review Import Mode system** (`importing/modes.py`) -- Replaces loose boolean flags with a typed, validated mode enum. This is a real API/behavioral change.

2. **Score-drop reassurance** (`score_update.py`) -- Coaching message when strict score goes down after fixes.

3. **Frozen plan-start score UX** (`queue_progress.py` + `status_cmd.py`) -- During active plan execution, shows the score frozen at plan start instead of fluctuating live scores, with queue countdown.

4. **`--confirm` convenience flag** (`override_handlers.py`) -- Shortcut for `plan done` that auto-generates attestation text from a note.

When I identified 9 features, the user said "Please work through all of them."

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Living plan system with queue_order, clusters, skipped items, overrides
   - Epic synthesis: LLM-driven meta-plan that clusters review findings by root cause
   - Synthesis stages: OBSERVE → REFLECT → ORGANIZE → COMMIT with stage gates
   - `synthesis::pending` queue item that blocks `next` command
   - Epic clusters: clusters with `epic/` prefix managed by synthesis engine
   - Plan version migration: v2 → v4 (adding synthesized_out skip kind, epic_synthesis_meta)
   - Frozen plan-start score: score snapshot taken at plan creation, displayed during active queue cycle instead of live deltas
   - `--confirm` flag: auto-generates attestation from `--note` text
   - Review preflight gates: blocking review reruns when backlog (objective + subjective) exists
   - Centralized queue policy: `build_plan_queue()` enforcing consistent defaults
   - ReviewImportMode: typed Literal replacing loose boolean flags

```
   117→def _migrate_deferred_to_skipped(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   118→    deferred: list[str] = plan["deferred"]
   119→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   120→    if not deferred:
   121→        return
   122→
   123→    now = utc_now()
   124→    for fid in list(deferred):
   125→        if fid in skipped:
   126→            continue
   127→        skipped[fid] = {

   129→            "kind": "temporary",
   130→            "reason": None,
   131→            "note": None,
   132→            "attestation": None,
   133→            "created_at": now,
   134→            "review_after": None,
   135→            "skipped_at_scan": 0,
   136→        }
   137→    deferred.clear()
   138→
   139→
   140→def _normalize_cluster_defaults(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   141→    for cluster in plan["clusters"].values():
   142→        if not isinstance(cluster, dict):
   143→            continue
   144→        if not isinstance(cluster.get("finding_ids"), list):
   145→            cluster["finding_ids"] = []
   146→        cluster.setdefault("auto", False)
   147→        cluster.setdefault("cluster_key", "")
   148→        cluster.setdefault("action", None)
   149→        cluster.setdefault("user_modified", False)
   150→
   151→
   152→def _migrate_epics_to_clusters(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   153→    """Migrate v3 top-level ``epics`` dict into ``clusters`` (v4 unification)."""
   154→    epics = plan.pop("epics", None)
   155→    if not isinstance(epics, dict) or not epics:
   156→        return
   157→    clusters = plan["clusters"]
   158→    now = utc_now()
   159→    for name, epic in epics.items():
   160→        if not isinstance(epic, dict):
   161→            continue
   162→        if name in clusters:
   163→            continue  # don't overwrite existing cluster
   164→        clusters[name] = {
   165→            "name": name,
   166→            "description": epic.get("thesis", ""),
   167→            "finding_ids": epic.get("finding_ids", []),
   168→            "auto": True,
   169→            "cluster_key": f"epic::{name}",
   170→            "action": f"desloppify plan focus {name}",
   171→            "user_modified": False,
   172→            "created_at": epic.get("created_at", now),
   173→            "updated_at": epic.get("updated_at", now),
   174→            # Epic-specific fields
   175→            "thesis": epic.get("thesis", ""),
   176→            "direction": epic.get("direction", "simplify"),
   177→            "root_cause": epic.get("root_cause", ""),
   178→            "supersedes": epic.get("supersedes", []),
   179→            "dismissed": epic.get("dismissed", []),
   180→            "agent_safe": epic.get("agent_safe", False),
   181→            "dependency_order": epic.get("dependency_order", 999),
   182→            "action_steps": epic.get("action_steps", []),
   183→            "source_clusters": epic.get("source_clusters", []),
   184→            "status": epic.get("status", "pending"),
   185→            "synthesis_version": epic.get("synthesis_version", 0),
   186→        }
   187→
   188→
   189→def ensure_plan_defaults(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   190→    """Normalize a loaded plan to ensure all keys exist.
   191→
   192→    Handles migration from v1 (deferred list) to v2 (skipped dict),
   193→    and v3 (top-level epics) to v4 (epics unified into clusters).
   194→    """
   195→    defaults = empty_plan()
   196→    for key, value in defaults.items():
   197→        plan.setdefault(key, value)
   198→
   199→    _ensure_container_types(plan)
   200→    _migrate_deferred_to_skipped(plan)
   201→    _migrate_epics_to_clusters(plan)
   202→    _normalize_cluster_defaults(plan)
   203→
   204→
   205→def synthesis_clusters(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Cluster]:
   206→    """Return clusters whose name starts with ``EPIC_PREFIX``."""
   207→    return {
   208→        name: cluster
   209→        for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items()
   210→        if name.startswith(EPIC_PREFIX)
   211→    }
   212→
   213→
   214→def validate_plan(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   215→    """Raise ValueError when plan invariants are violated."""
   216→    if not isinstance(plan.get("version"), int):
   217→        raise ValueError("plan.version must be an int")
   218→    if not isinstance(plan.get("queue_order"), list):
   219→        raise ValueError("plan.queue_order must be a list")
   220→
   221→    # No ID should appear in both queue_order and skipped
   222→    skipped_ids = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   223→    overlap = set(plan["queue_order"]) & skipped_ids
   224→    if overlap:
   225→        raise ValueError(
   226→            f"IDs cannot appear in both queue_order and skipped: {sorted(overlap)}"
   227→        )
   228→
   229→    # Validate skip entry kinds
   230→    for fid, entry in plan.get("skipped", {}).items():
   231→        kind = entry.get("kind")
   232→        if kind not in VALID_SKIP_KINDS:
   233→            raise ValueError(
   234→                f"Invalid skip kind {kind!r} for {fid}; must be one of {sorted(VALID_SKIP_KINDS)}"
   235→            )
   236→
   237→
   238→__all__ = [
```

```
     2→
     3→Clusters review findings by root cause, dismisses false positives,
     4→resolves contradictions, and orders by dependency. The synthesis is incremental:
     5→it updates existing synthesis-clusters rather than recreating from scratch.
     6→"""

     9→

    11→import logging

    29→
```
